Every browser test serves the run's one UI build (`test/e2e-build.ts`): `e2e/live.ts` (the real-session tests: setup, admin, AI, conversations and the rest) still compiled the UI through Vite per file, which on CI's two cores took most of each step's 60 s and summed past the admin test's 240 s. Service workers are blocked in every context. A timeout is raised only with a written reason, and in the describe option, not `vi.setConfig` (`forEachDriver(..., { timeout })`: SQL Server's describe option used to win over the file's 60 s). Routes in tests are NV-1's. |
| D608 | Done | HK-1: telemetry fields. Kept the hand-written sender (`log/appinsights.ts`, no SDK or dependency: the closed fields are the redaction boundary). Every request and every background job run is an operation (`log/operation.ts`, AsyncLocalStorage) with W3C ids: a request continues a valid incoming `traceparent`, else starts a trace; each job run starts its own. Every trace, dependency and exception made inside one carries its `ai.operation.id`, its span as parent, and its name. Requests are named `<METHOD> <route>`, `POST /api/action <action>` for a registered action. Jobs are request items with `kind=job`, the outcome as result code (ok, failed, partial, skipped), counts as measurements and short flags: `devops_check` (or `devops_reconcile` for a client's first check of the day: changed, removed, changes, calls; slowed yes or no; load full or changed), `requests_poll` and `requests_refresh` (polled, messages, candidates, promises, failures, conversations, unsorted, aiCalls: the AI pipeline's counts), `conversations_upkeep`, `people_refresh`. A client appears only as `clientRef(key)`, `c-` and 10 hex of a SHA-256 (a key is the client's Dataverse nickname or name); `focustime client-ref <key>` prints it to search by. Sampling stays at 100%. Expected ingest per month, from dev's and prod's last 3 days (about 25 MB each, about 0.25 GB a month, a third of it console logs) plus DevOps checks as jobs (about 2.5 KB a check with its trace and two dependencies; about 10 clients each minute in working hours): prod about 0.6 GB, dev about 0.3 GB (it sleeps outside use). Why: serverless billed the 0.5 vCore floor ($0.626 an hour) for every awake hour while the work was tiny (prod's busiest day 9.8 CPU seconds, worst minute 0.06 vCore; dev's worst minute, a migration, 0.09 vCore), so dev ran $6 to $7.50 a day and prod $6.60 on a full day; S0 is $0.4839 a day and S1 $0.9677 (Central US list, Nov 5). S0 is about 0.1 vCore and S1 about 0.2 (Microsoft's rule: 100 Standard DTU need at least 1 vCore); the app's pool is at most 10 against 60 and 90 workers. Lacking columnstore and in-memory OLTP doesn't matter (FocusTime uses neither). No app change: the tier switch drops connections for under 30 s and the driver already retries 40613, 40197 and dropped sockets for a minute. Point-in-time restore stays dev 7, prod 14. The idle rule (D431) and DV-1's outside-hours behavior stay: they still save container time and Graph and DevOps calls, though the database no longer pauses. S2 when DTU stays over 80% in working hours, for a large import or a restore (runbook). Rollback: revert the tier commit (sql.bicep's comment has the serverless values). |

## CV-4

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D710 | Done | CV-4 (Tanner's direction): FocusTime keeps no chat images. An image is read from Teams only when needed, always with a chat member's own delegated Chat.Read: on view (`GET /media/chat-images/<id>` keeps its session, member and client checks, FocusTime's own being the main gate, then the viewer's token), for the AI step, or for a DevOps create. On view the stripped bytes are held in memory only (`ImageCache`), 5 minutes per image and viewer (never shared between viewers), about 64 MB in all, the oldest out first; a failure is remembered 5 minutes too; the response is `no-store`. One retry on a throttle (Retry-After, 5 seconds at most). A refused token or a removed message is 404, and the page shows "Image in Teams" with Open in Teams (the message's deep link). The row keeps only the type and size read from the bytes, a skipped reason (not a PNG, JPEG or GIF, over 10 MB) and the AI's description. The poll no longer reads images. Replaces D623's storage. |
| D711 | Done | CV-4: the migration `20261107T1400_images_no_bytes` sets bytes NULL on rows that had them, puts them back to pending (attempts 0) and keeps their type and size; it adds `description` (200) and `described_at`. It logs only how many it cleared (`images_bytes_cleared`): named migrations take an optional `count` (both runners), as the numbered ones did. The bytes column is dropped in a later release (D468, Q-CV4-1); until then `store/images-bytes.test.ts` fails if any code writes it. Named 1400 on Nov 7 though the clock read Nov 5 14:00 UTC: it alters CV-2's table, so it must sort after `20261106T1020`, and main's newest was `20261107T1347`. |
| D712 | Done | CV-4: Admin › AI's per-client "Send attachments to AI" is a live Rule (`attachments_ai`, off by default). When it is on and AI may run for the client, the poll reads the batch's new images with the same token that read the chat, strips them, and adds them to the same `conversations.update` request: one user message, the input JSON, then each image after a fixed label written by code ("Image i1, in message m3:") as `input_image` (a data URL, `detail: "high"`), `store: false`. Because the batch is one JSON document, "after its message" is by label, not position. At most 4 a message and 10 a call; animated GIFs (OpenAI takes still ones only) and images already described aren't sent; the rest get no description. The service refuses anything but PNG, JPEG or GIF data URLs, or more than 10 (`bad_task`), before a request. The schema asks for `images` only when images go. Switch off, AI off, or the chat's daily cap: nothing is read or sent, only counted (`chat_images_ai` sent and not_sent). The guard reads text only and can't inspect images: the switch is the consent, and the Guide and Admin › AI say so. |
| D713 | Done | CV-4: an image's description is at most 120 characters, one line, with email addresses and secret shapes removed; an image ref the request didn't send is dropped (never stored), without rejecting the rest of the answer. Stored on the image row; shown on the card ("3 images: <the first description>") and under the thumbnail, only to members of the chat (the viewer's checks); never in logs, exports or Application Insights. A failed request stores none. |
| D714 | Done | CV-4: creating a DevOps item from a conversation or a promise (`conversation_create`) offers the images of the messages going with it, an internal chat's only with "include internal": the preview lists them as thumbnails with a checkbox each, all ticked (10 at most), and re-previews with `images` when one is unticked. On confirm, before the create, each ticked image is read with the creator's own token (the member check again), kept in memory only, stripped, and uploaded with the client's write PAT (`POST _apis/wit/attachments?fileName=&uploadType=simple`, `application/octet-stream`, 7.1); each becomes an AttachedFile relation in the same create patch with the comment "From Teams, <name>, <date time>", and the description gains "Attached: N images from the chat". The patch and an Attachments line per image (image id, file name, size, attachment id and URL, never bytes) are saved on the log entry before the create, so Retry never uploads again. An image that can't be read or uploaded is reported and left out; the item is still created. The read-back checks every relation. 10 MB an image, 10 a create. |
| D715 | Done | CV-4: undoing such a create sets it to Removed and removes its AttachedFile relations in the same patch (by index, highest first, under `test /rev`); the read-back checks they are gone. The undo's preview and result say the files stay in DevOps, unlinked: the attachments API has no delete. |
| D716 | Done | CV-4: cost. Image tokens are counted in the request's input tokens (the request log and the app's cap, as before). At the default gpt-6-luna prices ($0.10 per million input) and a patch multiplier of 1.2 (assumed, Q-CV4-3): a 1024 x 1024 screenshot is about 1,229 tokens, about $0.00012; a full-HD one about 2,448 tokens, $0.00025; a call's 10 images at most about $0.003. The test run (stub, 1,229 tokens an image) logged 2 images as 2,458 tokens beyond the text. |

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D717 | Done | AH-3: Ahead's layout follows the window width divided by the text scale (so Larger text at 1920 lays out like a narrower window): wide from 1400 (separate Start and Target), Dates from 1200 (one "Oct 19 → Nov 13" column), Readiness as dots with a tooltip and screen-reader words from 900, two-line cards below 900 and on the phone, with the filters wrapping. The Item column is at least 320 px, its title on one or two lines with the #id beside it; the other columns are compact and fixed (dates without the weekday, which is in the tooltip; the size on one line, "3 SP", with the field's name in the tooltip). The Gantt's label column uses the same rule (26% of the width, 240 to 440 px scaled). |

Each client can turn any of the four off with the client Rule `fix_gaps` in Admin › Clients (all on by default). |

Lower backlog items never show. An item never ranked in DevOps (no order value) isn't near the top. |

Fix next ignores Ahead's filters (it is its own queue); Skip still puts an item aside for the session. |

## GD-1

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D733 | Done | GD-1: a request whose client is Cooptimize Internal may name clients: the guard's three client rules (names and nicknames, DevOps projects, work item ids) don't apply to it, in what is sent or in what comes back. Secret shapes, the VM and outside email addresses still refuse it. Internal is known only by clientAccounts()'s why "internal" (`isInternalKey`, `actions/client-accounts.ts`), never by its name; `GuardInput.internal` is optional, so `guard()`'s callers keep working. |
| D734 | Done | GD-1: an AI answer passes the client rule too (the request's client, Internal exempt). A refused answer was sent and paid for: logged as refused with its tokens, never shown, never retried ("The AI's answer mentions another client, so nothing is shown"). Only the client rule is checked on answers: an outside email address or a secret shape in an answer can only come from an image (the text was guarded), and CV-4 already scrubs those from what it keeps; refusing the whole answer for them would leave every chat with such a screenshot unsorted. |
| D735 | Done | GD-1: before any write into a client's DevOps (create, Break down's stories, comment, a state change's comment, description), the text FocusTime would write (title, description, acceptance criteria, repro steps, comment) is checked against the person's other clients; one named refuses the preview with "This mentions <other client>; remove it before saving to <client>'s DevOps" (code `other_client`). The other client's name appears only in that response, never in a log or the audit log (nothing is written). Internal's DevOps may name clients. Undos only put back what was there and aren't checked. |
| D736 | Done | GD-1: a guard refusal logs one line (`ai guard`, kind `<task>:<input or output>:<rule>`, and the matched client entry as its one-way client ref), never the text, so a refusal can be traced to the rule and the client (`focustime client-ref <key>`). Client keys compare without case in the guard, so a client's own names never count as another client's. |
| D737 | Done | GD-1: chat messages the guard refused before GD-1 (reason "refused_client", which caught every Internal chat) are tried once more on the chat's next poll, as their own batch so they never hold back new messages; Sort unsorted also retries them. Refusals from GD-1 on are stored as "other_client" (same words in Inbox), so a real mention is retried only by Sort unsorted, never on every poll. |
| D738 | Done | TB-1: the bot uses the Teams SDK 2.1.1 (`@microsoft/teams.apps`, `.api`, `.cards`, exact versions, lockfile committed), Microsoft's framework for Teams-only apps; the Bot Framework SDK is retired (support ended Dec 31, 2025) and the Microsoft 365 Agents SDK is for agents across several surfaces. Its packages are used only in `server/teams/`. It is mounted on FocusTime's own server through its adapter seam: `app.initialize()`, never `app.start()`, no second port (Tanner's OK, Nov 5). |
| D739 | Done | TB-1: the SDK's `@azure/msal-node` 5.6.0 has a high advisory (GHSA-xcx6-jx8p-x3h5, in the authorization code flow, which the bot never uses). An npm override points it at 7.1.2, the patched copy `@azure/identity` already brings; `npm audit` is clean and the SDK's paths the bot uses (a token provider, no MSAL) are unchanged. |
| D740 | Done | TB-1: the bot's credential is a user-assigned managed identity (`ft-<env>-bot-id`, msaAppType UserAssignedMSI, single tenant), not a client secret: nothing in Key Vault, nothing to rotate, no new app registration. The "FocusTime" registration stays for sign-in only. All of it is Bicep through the normal deploys (Tanner, Nov 5). |
| D741 | Done | TB-1: incoming activities are verified by the SDK (Bot Framework OpenID keys, issuer `https://api.botframework.com`, audience the bot's app id, RS256, expiry with 5 minutes' skew, the `serviceurl` claim matching the activity); a failure is a 401 before FocusTime reads anything. The SDK doesn't check key endorsements; FocusTime's gate requires `channelId` msteams, pins Cooptimize's tenant (the SDK's Entra-token path takes the tenant from the token), and sends replies only to Teams' connector hosts. |
| D742 | Done | TB-1: who may talk to the bot: the sender's Entra object id must be a FocusTime user (signed in once, not deleted, not a guest UPN) and a Cooptimizer by `cooptimizers()` (no guests, no automation accounts, inside the domains Rule). Others get "FocusTime is for Cooptimize accounts."; someone not signed in yet gets a link to sign in once; a group chat or channel gets "FocusTime only works in 1:1 chats" (the manifest is personal scope only, so this is a guard). |
| D743 | Done | TB-1: the SDK's logger is replaced by one that drops every argument (it would log whole activities at debug); only `teams_sdk warn|error` reach the log. Bot records carry a kind and counts, never text, people, conversation ids, tokens or headers (a test sends a message and checks the log). |
| D744 | Done | TB-1: Teams waits 10 to 15 seconds and dev takes about 30 to wake from zero. The endpoint answers 200 as soon as the token and gate pass, then sends typing, the reply, and "On it…" first when the assistant takes over 2.5 s; Teams' redeliveries are handled once by activity id. One person's turns run one at a time in the order they arrived, so replies come back in order (one replica). On dev the first message after it sleeps may be late or lost (send it again; documented); dev stays at zero (Tanner, Nov 5). |
| D745 | Done | TB-1: a proposal card is Adaptive Card 1.5 with the preview (title, who sees it, each field before → after, an optional note) and Confirm and Cancel as `Action.Execute` (an `Action.Submit` fallback). The row in `teams_bot_cards` is written before the card is sent; a tap claims it (open → running) in one UPDATE, so `assistant.confirm` runs once however often it is tapped and later taps get the stored outcome. Outcomes: Done (with Undo and links into the app), Couldn't do it (with the reason), Expired (15 minutes, AS-1's expiry), Cancelled, or, on `changed`, the new preview on the same card. A confirm over 8 s answers "Working on it…" and updates the card when it finishes. |
| D746 | Done | TB-1: quick commands without AI: what's next and today (the person's own `week.load` and the Now strip rule, so they match Today), help, open FocusTime. Everything else goes to AS-1's `handle`; AI off for FocusTime or a client returns the assistant's own words, the same as the panel. Until AS-1 merges the bot is wired with no assistant and says what it can do. |
| D747 | Done | TB-1: the 1:1 chat's address (conversation id, service URL, tenant) is kept from the first message or install (`teams_bot_chats`, a user table, removed on uninstall) for later proactive steps (NT-1, a morning brief), which will go through the notification gate. "Let FocusTime message me in Teams" (`[notify] teams_messages`, Settings › Notifications › In Teams) is off by default; nothing reads it to send in TB-1. Both bot tables are left out of the export (Teams' plumbing, rebuilt by the next message), so the export format doesn't change. |
| D748 | Done | TB-1: the Teams app package (`scripts/teams-package.ts`): manifest 1.30, a fixed app id per environment (an upload replaces, never adds), name "FocusTime" (dev "FocusTime (dev)"), the WeekMark icons (192 color, 32 white outline), the bot in the personal scope only, no tabs, files, SSO or Graph permissions. Tanner uploads prod in the Teams admin center; dev goes to testers through a setup policy. The privacy and terms links point at the app's About (an org-only app; no public pages). |
| D749 | Done | TB-1: `Microsoft.BotService` wasn't registered in the subscription and the deploy identity can't register providers, so `infra/scripts/bootstrap.sh` (step 1) now registers it; Tanner runs it (dry run first) once before the first deploy with the bot. |

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D750 | Done | AS-1: one assistant service (`assistant/service.ts`) for two front ends: the in-app panel (the registry's `assistant.*` actions) and TB-1's Teams bot (`server/assistant.ts`'s hub, by the person's Entra object id; the server builds everything else). The interface is locked in `assistant/types.ts` and `docs/assistant-interface.md`; a change is listed there with its date. |
| D751 | Done | AS-1: everything the assistant can do is one list (`assistant/allowlist.ts`): six reads (what's next, a day, an item, gaps, commitments, sends), eleven proposals (move, drop or keep a pick, confirm the day or week, add a must, an Ahead edit, a send, a DevOps comment, a description, a capture) and the close-the-loop stub ("Coming with LP-1"). Its dispatch runs only the actions in that list and what they read (`RUNS`); secrets, settings, admin, sign-in, data, the VM, the timer and the assistant's own actions are never reachable (`NEVER`, checked by a test). |
| D752 | Done | AS-1: injected instructions can't make a write. The first round (intent) sees only my words and the screen's ids, never chat, DevOps or capture text, and names the proposals my words ask for. Reads are always allowed; a proposal only when that round named it. The provider gets `allowed_tools` and `ai/service.ts runTools` refuses any other name without running it. An item id must come from my words, the screen or a read's structured fields; a send's recipient must be named in my words. Read text goes back to the model as quoted data, never as instructions. |
| D753 | Done | AS-1: one client per request, by the guard's own rule (`ai/guard.ts clientsNamed`: names, nicknames, DevOps projects of 3+ letters, known item ids) with GD-1's rule for Internal (clientAccounts()'s why "internal", never a name; keys without case). Two clients named is "Which client?" and nothing is sent. A request naming no client may read across my clients, but then the model hears only "N cards shown", never their facts. |
| D754 | Done | AS-1: every write is a proposal (the same preview the button shows, who sees it, before and after), stored 15 minutes in `assistant_proposals`. Confirm re-checks in order: mine and open, not expired, still on the allowlist, the client still mine and the proposal's, then a fresh preview compared with the one shown (DevOps: revision and before values; FocusTime: the pick or row). Any difference returns `changed` with the fresh proposal. Then it dispatches the same action the button calls, which does its own permission check. Edits only to the fields the proposal lists (a comment's text, a description's after, a pick's day and half). |
| D755 | Done | AS-1: a new named DevOps write, `description_edit`: only on items the shared `canEdit` allows, always the whole text before → after in the preview, `test /rev`, read back, the old text in the audit log, undo puts it back and is refused when the description changed since. CV-1's `description_update` still only appends. The comment write is step 14's `comment`. |

A send goes through SW-1's `send.create` with its rules (Must only for a client's leads; the refusal is passed through). |
| D757 | Done | AS-1: three new reads, each on the allowlist as a read: `assistant.today` (what's next, due today, due tonight), `lookup.day` (my own picks, focus and resume note for a day) and `items.get` (only items on my clients, through Ahead's items). |
| D758 | Done | AS-1: history is two user tables (`assistant_turns`, `assistant_proposals`; a timestamped migration), kept 30 days rolling, cleared from Settings › Data (Assistant history › Clear), in Export my data and never shown to admins. Proposals older than a day after they settle or expire go sooner. The export's schema version stays 28: it follows the frozen numbered migrations (`store/import.test.ts`), the new tables only add, and an older build's import reads only the tables it knows (TB-1's tables aren't exported, so it didn't bump either). |
| D759 | Done | AS-1: voice is push-to-talk only (hold, or tap to start and tap to stop), 60 seconds and 2 MB at most, WebM or MP4 audio, sent to OpenAI's `gpt-transcribe` ($0.0045 a minute; the transcriptions endpoint keeps no data) through `ai/` with the caps and the request log (task `assistant_transcribe`, cost on whole seconds, never text). The audio is held in memory for the one request and never stored or logged; the transcript fills the box to edit and is sent only with Send. The gate: AI for FocusTime on and, when a client is in focus (the screen's client or the selected item's), that client's Allow AI; otherwise the mic is off with "Voice is off while <client> is in focus". `Permissions-Policy` now allows the microphone for FocusTime's own pages only (`microphone=(self)`). |
| D760 | Done | AS-1: the panel opens with Ctrl+J (Cmd+J), / when no text field has focus, or the top bar's Ask (a speech mark on the phone); Esc and × close it. A side panel at the right like the detail panel (440 px, 560 at 3840); on the phone a bottom sheet resting on the bottom bar with a full-width Hold to talk button. Answers are cards (item, block, pick, send, promise, note) with links into the app; proposals have Confirm, Edit and Cancel inline, and a DevOps write's Undo opens the write framework's undo preview. With AI off or not set up the panel says so and opens the command menu. |
| D761 | Done | AS-1: the demo gets a scripted assistant (`sources/demo/assistant.ts`: a few plain requests to the same tool calls, a fixed transcript, no network) used only by the assistant, so the demo shows lookups and proposals through the real service; the demo's other AI features stay off. The demo still can't preview DevOps writes (no writer), so the report's comment-preview screenshot uses the write framework's own words for a demo item. |
| D762 | Done | AS-1 into TB-1: the bot keeps its own small types and gets `teamsAssistant()` (`server/teams/assistant.ts`) over the hub: cards become a title, lines and absolute links, proposals keep their rows, who sees it and expiry, ids are strings in the bot and numbers in the service, and a DevOps write's Undo links to Settings › Data. The bot sends only `{ screen: "teams" }` as context. |
| D763 | Done | TB-1b: the Bot Connector's endorsement rule, which the Teams SDK 2.1.1 skips: a token for an incoming activity must be signed by a Bot Framework key whose `endorsements` include the activity's channel, and FocusTime takes only `msteams`. `server/teams/endorsements.ts` runs before the SDK's check, from the same keys address (kept a day; fetched again early for an unknown key at most every 5 minutes; none kept and none fetchable means refused); 401 and `auth teams_token not_endorsed` otherwise. Only Bot Framework's issuer passes: the SDK would also take Entra-issued tokens (Agent 365 agent identities), which the Teams channel never sends this bot. The tenant pin stays. |
| D764 | Done | TB-1b: handled activities are recorded in `teams_bot_seen` (a user table: a SHA-256 of Teams' activity id and when, never the activity) with a unique insert (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, a `MERGE WITH (HOLDLOCK)` on SQL Server), so a message Teams delivers again is answered once whichever replica gets it; rows older than a day go as new ones come. The in-memory check stays in front (it also covers refusals, which have no person). The store check runs in the background turn, after Teams has its 200, so the answer never waits on the database. If the store can't be reached the message is answered (a rare double answer beats none). Taps were already safe across replicas (the card claim). |
| D765 | Done | TB-1b: one person's turns keep their order in memory, which assumes one replica. Prod's max replicas is 1 (Bicep and live, Nov 5, Single revision mode); during a deploy the old and new revisions overlap for seconds, when two quick messages could be answered by both. Raising max replicas would move the order to the store first. |
| D766 | Done | TB-1b: Delete my data and Clear remove the bot's rows at once (`recordSoftDelete`: `teams_bot_chats`, `teams_bot_cards`, `teams_bot_seen`); the rest of the person's data waits the 30 days as before, and the hard delete and an admin's Remove take every user table, these included. They are left out of the export because they hold no content of the person's: a technical address of their 1:1 chat (conversation id, service URL, tenant, the bot's id), card state (a proposal id, its title, the outcome card) and hashes of handled activity ids, meaningful only to this tenant's bot and rebuilt by their next message. Clear in Settings › Data (Assistant history, AS-1) removes them too, in the same transaction as the assistant's turns and proposals (the cards are the same history from Teams; the count it shows stays the assistant's). |
| D767 | Done | TB-1b: the Teams manifest's privacy and terms links go to the app's own page, `/legal.html#privacy` and `#terms` (`public/legal.html`, a plain document with its own stylesheet, no script, served without signing in like the app's other static files): what FocusTime reads and keeps, the bot, AI, export and delete, and short terms of use for Cooptimize staff. Replaces the About link (D748). |

## TB-2

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D837 | Done | TB-2 (amends TB-1's rule, approved Nov 5): the bot sends only in the person's own 1:1 chat: replies, card updates, and, with "Let FocusTime message me in Teams" and that kind's switch on, the brief, check-in, close out and nudges, through the notification gate. Every write it offers is a proposal card that runs only on Confirm, except the person's own plan-store changes from an explicit button tap or "capture:", which run at once with Undo (the same actions as the app's buttons). Never DevOps, Outlook or anything external without Confirm. The glossary's "never messages first" now says this. |
| D838 | Done | TB-2: four kinds, each its own switch under the master switch, all off by default (`[notify] teams_brief`, `teams_checkin`, `teams_closeout`, `teams_nudges`; `teams_friday`, `teams_brief_at` "start" or HH:MM, `teams_checkin_at`, `teams_closeout_minutes` 5 to 120). The brief at the day's first segment (or the set time) until the morning ends or an hour after; the check-in at 12:00 until two hours before the day ends or an hour after; close out 30 minutes before the day ends until it ends; each clamped to the person's hours. Past its window a kind is skipped, never sent late. Monday to Thursday; Friday only with Also on Fridays; never weekends or PTO days (`plan/teams-rhythm.ts`). |
| D839 | Done | TB-2: the notification gate decides (`plan/notify.ts` `gate()`, the same facts the notifier uses): quiet hours wait, focus holds, and a kind that waited goes once that's over if its window is still open. The idle rule's wake includes the next rhythm time (`nextRhythmWake`), every 5 minutes while one waits. |
| D840 | Done | TB-2: at most once. Each occasion (`brief:<date>`, `checkin:<date>`, `closeout:<date>`, `nudges:<hour>`, `nudge:<key>`, `test:<kind>:<ms>`) is claimed by a unique insert in `teams_rhythm` (a user table, `store/migrations/20261111T0900_teams_rhythm.ts`, left out of the export like the other bot tables) before the send, so two replicas (a deploy's overlap) send it once. A send Teams refuses is marked failed; a claim with no message id after 10 minutes is swept to lost and logged; neither is ever sent again. Rows are kept 30 days. |
| D841 | Done | TB-2: dev stays at zero replicas (option A): no cron scale rule. A scheduled kind goes only while dev is awake and inside its window, else it's skipped. Testing on dev is Settings › Notifications › In Teams › Send me a test now, which sends that kind at once (marked "(test)") past the switches and the gate; nudges use a sample when nothing is due. |
| D842 | Done | TB-2: send pacing (`pacedSender`): at most 10 sends a second across the app (Microsoft's limits are 7 a second per bot per chat and 50 a second per app per tenant; one person's sends are rarely more than one), and 429, 412, 502 and 504 are retried 3 times with exponential backoff from 2 s up to 20 s with ±20% jitter, or Retry-After when Teams gives one. Cards are sent alone, never text and a card together (message ids can go missing then). |

## WK-1

Numbered by HK-4.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D850 | Done | WK-1 (amends D383, D404 and UI-8's panel entry): Week's Board picks one tier for the whole grid from the real width of a Mon–Thu column (the grid's content box after the detail panel's space and the gaps, measured with a ResizeObserver and again on a Text size change) divided by the text scale: Rail ≥ 320 px (the rail with titles, 186 px down to 140), Ruler 250 to 320 (a slim 60 px ruler: hour ticks and event bars, titles on hover and in the panel), Strip 170 to 250 (no rail; one thin day strip under the card header: off hours, free time, events, today's now tick), Mini 130 to 170 (the strip; the header stacked, client up to two lines then the time; the status pill reads "At risk" alone, its reason in the title and the panel; no size chips; no date or Today pill in the day header). The tier is chosen in JS (`ui/weekfit.ts`) and the card renders exactly that tier's element (`railFor` in `ui/layout.ts`); the card container queries at 380, 300 and 230 px and the dead 76 and 46 px rules are removed. Rail starts at 320 rather than the 330 first proposed, so a 1440 px window with no panel (324 px columns) keeps the rail with titles. The rail's width is set from the measured column (`--rail-w`: the column less about 200 px of text, scaled, 140 to 186 px); U5.1's `clamp(96px, 100cqw - 250px, 186px)` is removed, since a card can't query its own width and the rule left the rail at about 84 px. Why the tier is chosen in JS: a CSS-hidden rail left the card with neither the rail's now line nor the no-rail one (the now line vanished with the panel open under 1600). |
| D851 | Done | WK-1: when even Mini doesn't fit with all five days open, the week folds: the selected day (or the day being planned) and its neighbours stay open as normal columns, kept inside the week at its ends, and the other days become 44 px strips (scaled with text): weekday and date, the AM and PM halves in the client's colour, each its own drop target with the usual fit tip and highlighted under a dragged story, the at-risk (or watch) dot, PTO hatched, Catch-up dashed, and today's now line. A click on a strip, or the arrow keys moving the selection, opens that day and moves the window. Fewer than three days stay open only when three would still be under Mini (1024 with the panel at Larger text). The fit tip on a strip opens towards the open days, so it never runs past the week's edge. |
| D852 | Done | WK-1: the whole week shows from 1024 px (the medium layout's floor was 1134); 600 to 1023 keeps the one-day layout and the phone stays under 600 px, both unchanged. |
| D853 | Done | WK-1: a PTO day takes Friday's light share (0.6), and the light share has no pixel floor, so Friday (or a PTO day) is never wider than a working day; the 190 px Friday minimum is gone. |
| D854 | Done | WK-1 (amends UI-8): below 1440 px the detail panel is `clamp(360px, 30vw, 440px)` wide (440 from 1440, 560 from 3000); the grid still keeps clear of it. A day header never spills into the next column: it clips, and its date gives way under 17em and the Today pill under 12em (container queries in em, so Larger text gives way sooner), besides UI-8's rule with the panel open under 1600. |
| D855 | Done | WK-1: titles wrap to two lines before they're cut: client names, picks, suggested cards and their origins, the status pill, rail event titles, PTO and catch-up lines. The card header has a min-height instead of a fixed height and wraps, so the time range takes its own line instead of hiding under the pill; a block's time always shows. |
| D856 | Done | WK-1: the detail panel belongs to the Board: it is hidden on Team (where it covered the list and took no space) and back, as it was, on return to the Board. Plan already hid it. |
| D857 | Done | WK-1: the top bar stays one row from 1024 px: under 1280 the word FocusTime gives way to the mark (kept as the button's accessible name) and the VM chip shows its dot and one word (Running, Stopped, Billing, Starting, Stopping, Unknown; the full text in its title and menu). Plan's week grid: on a cell narrower than 11em, "Planning" becomes an accent dot so the client's name keeps its room. |
| D858 | Done | WK-1: test note. Playwright's drag sends no dragover while the pointer rests, and Chromium sends a dragleave with no related target on entering a child, so a resting synthetic drag can lose the hover mark; `e2e/wk1.test.ts` nudges the pointer. Real browsers keep sending dragover, so the product is unchanged. A PTO day answers a drag with "Can't drop here", as before (PV-1). |

One read-only action, `diagnostics.check`, nothing logged beyond the usual request line, nothing stored |

My conversations by status, and each "committed" one by reason, decided by the two facts that disagree: the status line names the first open commitment's first name, while Promises lists commitments whose `who_id` is my sign-in id. Reasons: in Promises (no gap), someone else committed (no gap), no open commitment (a stale status), no person id, my name under another person id (a second account or a guest copy), another FocusTime user with my first name, and my promise exists but Promises and Needs you leave it out (snoozed, hidden, shown as a follow-up). The gaps list my own conversation's client and title, never a message |

Message text is read on the server only to test the phrase, as the pipeline does, and never returned, logged or kept. Retry extraction is offered only where the reason passes on its own (`unsortedLine(...).does === "retry"`) and runs the existing Sort unsorted |
